# What came first? — the owner's review sheet

Generated by [build-what-came-first.ts](../../../scripts/build-what-came-first.ts) from the hand-kept files in [src/data/what-came-first/](../../../src/data/what-came-first/); do not edit by hand. Every item and every order fact carries the verse that settles it, checked against the site's own Berean text. To strike a pair, add it to that category's blocked list in its source file and rebuild.

## 1. Who lived first?

Who's older?'s 160 people, their windows, facts and blocked pairs, unchanged: see [its review sheet](../whos-older/review-sheet.md). The build checked that the new game asks exactly the same 10,789 pairs, each with the same answer, reason and difficulty.

## 2. What happened first?

The Timeline sort game's events, windows and order facts, unchanged. 248 events, 28,052 askable pairs (2,332 easy, 10,738 medium, 14,982 hard).

### This game's own blocks

- **the Creation week** (never asked against each other: God creates the heavens and the earth, God forms Adam from the dust of the ground, God makes Eve from Adam's rib): The order of what God made in the Creation week is left out of this game (owner, 2026-11-12).
- **Jesus's miracles** (never asked against each other: Jesus turns water into wine at Cana, Jesus heals an official's son from Cana, Jesus calms a storm on the lake, Jesus feeds the five thousand, Jesus walks on the water, Jesus raises Lazarus from the dead): Which of Jesus's miracles came first is left out (owner, 2026-11-12): the gospels often do not settle the order. His first sign (John 2:11) is a Bible First instead.
- **the plagues** (never asked against each other: The Nile turns to blood, Egypt's firstborn die on Passover night): The order of the plagues is left out of this game (owner, 2026-11-12).
- **Hezekiah reopens the temple doors** and **Samaria falls to Assyria**: Rests on Hezekiah's first year: 729 BC counting a shared reign with Ahaz (Thiele), but 715 BC on Albright's dates, after Samaria fell.
- **Mary anoints Jesus' feet at Bethany** and **Jesus rides into Jerusalem on a donkey**: John puts the anointing the day before the entry (John 12:1, 12), but Matthew and Mark tell the Bethany anointing after it (Matthew 26:6; Mark 14:3).

The Timeline game's 47 contested pairs are carried over as blocked pairs too (listed below).

## 3. Who ruled first?

95 rulers, 4,194 askable pairs (583 easy, 653 medium, 2,958 hard). "First" means began to rule first. Every window is worked out from the site's ruler pages: the start year in every dating system given, the end year of the ruler before, then one year more at each end. 20 successions are already settled by the windows and need no fact.

### Joshua and the judges before Saul (the one fact that reaches more than one pair)

Joshua, Othniel, Ehud, Shamgar, Deborah, Gideon, Abimelech, Tola, Jair, Jephthah, Ibzan, Elon, Abdon, Samson, Eli, Samuel have no years. Acts 13:19–21: "After the conquest of Canaan, God gave Israel judges until the time of Samuel, and only then a king, Saul. Every later ruler began after Saul." So each is asked against Saul and against every ruler who certainly began after Saul (a window wholly after his, or a verse that puts them after him): 78 rulers, 1,248 pairs.

<details>
<summary>The rulers they reach</summary>

Saul, Ish-bosheth, David, Solomon, Jeroboam I, Nadab, Baasha, Elah, Zimri, Omri, Ahab, Ahaziah of Israel, Joram of Israel, Jehu, Jehoahaz of Israel, Jehoash of Israel, Jeroboam II, Zechariah, king of Israel, Shallum, Menahem, Pekahiah, Pekah, Hoshea, Rehoboam, Abijah, Asa, Jehoshaphat, Jehoram of Judah, Ahaziah of Judah, Athaliah, Joash of Judah, Amaziah, Uzziah, Jotham, Ahaz, Hezekiah, Manasseh, Amon, Josiah, Jehoahaz of Judah, Jehoiakim, Jehoiachin, Zedekiah, Shishak, Tirhakah, Pharaoh Neco, Hophra, Tiglath-pileser, Shalmaneser, Sargon, Sennacherib, Esarhaddon, Merodach-baladan, Nebuchadnezzar, Evil-merodach, Belshazzar, Darius the Mede, Cyrus, Darius (Ezra 6), Ahasuerus, Artaxerxes, Nehemiah, Gedaliah, Herod the Great, Archelaus, Herod Antipas, Philip the tetrarch, Herod Agrippa I, Agrippa II, Caesar Augustus, Quirinius, Tiberius Caesar, Pontius Pilate, Claudius, Sergius Paulus, Gallio, Felix, Porcius Festus

</details>

### Facts a dating system contradicts (refused, and the pair blocked)

- Ahab before Jehoshaphat (dated by the other king's reign): Albright has Jehoshaphat 873 BC before Ahab 869 BC.
- Joram of Israel before Jehoram of Judah (dated by the other king's reign): Thiele–McFall has Jehoram of Judah 854 BC before Joram of Israel 852 BC.
- Pekah before Jotham (dated by the other king's reign): Albright has Jotham 750 BC before Pekah 737 BC.

### Blocked pairs

| Pair never asked | Why |
|---|---|
| Ahab and Jehoshaphat | 1 Kings 22:41 puts Jehoshaphat's accession in Ahab's fourth year, and Thiele–McFall and Ussher agree, but Albright dates Jehoshaphat (873 BC) before Ahab (869 BC). |
| Pekahiah and Pekah | 2 Kings 15:27 places Pekah after Pekahiah, but Thiele–McFall counts Pekah's twenty years from a rival reign beginning in 752 BC, before Pekahiah. |
| Menahem and Pekah | Thiele–McFall begins Pekah's rival reign in 752 BC, the year Menahem began. |
| Pekah and Jotham | 2 Kings 15:32 dates Jotham by Pekah's second year, but Albright dates Pekah (737 BC) after Jotham (750 BC). |
| Jehoram of Judah and Joram of Israel | 2 Kings 1:17 and 8:16 date them by each other in opposite directions (Jehoram of Judah's shared reign with his father), and the dating systems disagree. |
| Ish-bosheth and David | Both began in the year Saul died on both dated systems, and 2 Samuel 2:10–11 gives Ish-bosheth two years against David's seven and a half in Hebron, so which began first is uncertain. |
| Darius the Mede and Cyrus | Who Darius the Mede was is disputed (some identify him with Cyrus himself, others with the governor Gubaru), so their order over Babylon cannot be settled in one line. |
| Samson and Jephthah | Judges 10:7 has the Philistine and Ammonite oppressions begin together, and many chronologies overlap Samson with Jephthah and those after him. |
| Samson and Ibzan | Samson's twenty years may overlap Jephthah's successors (Judges 10:7). |
| Samson and Elon | Samson's twenty years may overlap Jephthah's successors (Judges 10:7). |
| Samson and Abdon | Samson's twenty years may overlap Jephthah's successors (Judges 10:7). |
| Samson and Eli | Many chronologies place Samson's judgeship within Eli's forty years. |
| Shamgar and Deborah | Judges 3:31 says 'After Ehud came Shamgar', but Judges 5:6 places Shamgar's days alongside Jael's, so his order against Deborah is unclear. |
| Ahasuerus and Darius (Ezra 6) | The page dates Ahasuerus as Xerxes I, but Ussher makes Esther's Ahasuerus Darius I and Josephus makes the Ahasuerus of Ezra 4:6 Cambyses, so the order changes with the identification. |
| Ahasuerus and Artaxerxes | The Greek Esther makes Ahasuerus Artaxerxes, so the order changes with the identification. |
| Herod the Great and Caesar Augustus | Augustus's page dates him from the title in 27 BC, but Octavian ruled from 43 or 31 BC, before Herod was made king (40 BC). 'Began to rule' has two answers. |
| Agrippa II and Felix | Felix's page dates his governorship from AD 52, but Tacitus has him governing Samaria from about AD 48, when Agrippa II was given Chalcis (AD 48–50). |

### Left out

- **Pharaoh of Joseph's day, of the oppression, of the Exodus, and Solomon's father-in-law**: The brief asks for Pharaohs named in Scripture; these four are never named.
- **So, king of Egypt**: No years on his page and no verse that orders him against another ruler.
- **Nero**: Scripture never names him (Paul appeals to 'Caesar', Acts 25:11); the brief asks for Caesars named in Scripture.
- **Hiram, Hazael and Rezin**: Dated on one system only (the main system's dates run about 40 years after Ussher's); Rezin's year is his first mention in Assyrian records, not his accession; Hiram against David is the known problem of 2 Samuel 5:11.
- **The three Ben-hadads, Mesha, the queen of Sheba, Candace and Lysanias**: No usable years on their pages and no verse that orders them against enough other rulers.
- **Zerubbabel**: His page's start is the return from exile, not a term of office: Sheshbazzar was Cyrus's governor (Ezra 5:14) and Zerubbabel is first called governor in Darius's second year (Haggai 1:1), so when he began to govern is unknown.
- **Vashti and Esther**: Queens consort, not rulers.

## 4. What was built first?

### Order facts

| Came first | Then | Verse | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cain's city | Noah's ark | Genesis 4:17; Genesis 5:32 | Cain built his city when his son Enoch, Adam's grandson, was born, while Noah, nine generations after Adam, built the ark after he was 500 (Genesis 4:17; 5:32). |
| Cain's city | Noah's altar | Genesis 7:23; Genesis 4:17; Genesis 8:20 | Cain built his city before the Flood, which left alive only Noah and those with him in the ark, and Noah built his altar after coming out of the ark (Genesis 7:23; 8:20). |
| Cain's city | Tower of Babel | Genesis 7:23; Genesis 4:17; Genesis 10:32 | Cain built his city before the Flood, which left alive only Noah and those with him in the ark, and Babel was built by Noah's descendants after the flood (Genesis 7:23; 10:32). |
| Noah's ark | Noah's altar | Genesis 8:18–20 | Noah built the altar after he, his family and the animals came out of the ark (Genesis 8:18-20). |
| Noah's ark | Tower of Babel | Genesis 10:32; Genesis 11:1–9 | The ark was built before the Flood, and Babel was built after Noah's descendants had spread out after the flood (Genesis 10:32; 11:1-9). |
| Noah's altar | Tower of Babel | Genesis 10:32; Genesis 8:18–20 | Noah built his altar as soon as he came out of the ark, but Babel came after his sons' families had grown into nations after the flood (Genesis 8:18-20; 10:32). |
| Abram's altar at Shechem | Abram's altar at Hebron | Genesis 13:18; Genesis 12:7; Genesis 13:1 | Abram built the Shechem altar when he first came into Canaan, and the Hebron altar after he had gone down to Egypt and come back (Genesis 12:7; 13:1, 18). |

| Ebenezer stone | Saul's first altar | 1 Samuel 8:4–5; 1 Samuel 7:12; 1 Samuel 14:35 | Samuel set up Ebenezer before the elders, finding him old, asked for a king, and Saul built his altar as that king (1 Samuel 7:12; 8:4-5; 14:35). |
| Ebenezer stone | David's palace | 1 Samuel 8:4–5; 2 Samuel 5:9–11 | Samuel set up Ebenezer before Israel asked for a king, and David built his palace after Saul's reign, once he had taken Jerusalem (1 Samuel 8:4-5; 2 Samuel 5:9-11). |
| Ebenezer stone | Altar at Araunah's threshing floor | 1 Samuel 8:4–5; 2 Samuel 24:18; 2 Samuel 24:25 | Samuel set up Ebenezer before Israel asked for a king, and David built the altar on Araunah's threshing floor while he was king (1 Samuel 8:4-5; 2 Samuel 24:18, 25). |
| Gideon's altar | Saul's first altar | 1 Samuel 12:11–12; 1 Samuel 14:35 | When Saul was made king, Samuel spoke of the LORD sending Jerubbaal (Gideon) as something already past, and Saul built his altar as king (1 Samuel 12:11-12; 14:35). |
| Saul's first altar | David's palace | 2 Samuel 5:3–5; 2 Samuel 5:9–11 | Saul built his altar while he was king, and David built his palace after Saul's death, once all Israel had made him king and he had taken Jerusalem (2 Samuel 5:3-11). |
| Saul's first altar | Altar at Araunah's threshing floor | 2 Samuel 5:3–5; 2 Samuel 24:18; 2 Samuel 24:25 | Saul built his altar while he was king, and David built the altar on Araunah's threshing floor in Jerusalem as king after Saul (2 Samuel 5:3-5; 24:18, 25). |
| David's palace | Solomon's Temple | 2 Samuel 7:1–2 | David was already living in his house of cedar when he told Nathan the ark of God was still in a tent, before the temple was built (2 Samuel 7:1-2). |
| David's palace | Solomon's palace | 1 Kings 9:10; 2 Samuel 5:11; 1 Kings 2:10–12 | David's palace was built in his own reign, and Solomon finished his palace after David's death, at the end of twenty years of building (2 Samuel 5:11; 1 Kings 2:10-12; 9:10). |
| David's palace | Jeroboam's golden calves | 1 Kings 11:42–43; 2 Samuel 5:11 | David's palace was built in David's reign, and Jeroboam made his calves only after Solomon had reigned forty years and died (2 Samuel 5:11; 1 Kings 11:42-43). |
| Altar at Araunah's threshing floor | Solomon's Temple | 2 Chronicles 3:1; 2 Samuel 24:25 | Solomon built the temple on the threshing floor David had already prepared, where David had built his altar (2 Chronicles 3:1; 2 Samuel 24:25). |
| Altar at Araunah's threshing floor | Solomon's palace | 1 Kings 9:10; 1 Kings 2:10–12; 2 Samuel 24:25 | David built the altar in his own lifetime, and Solomon finished his palace after David's death, at the end of twenty years of building (2 Samuel 24:25; 1 Kings 2:10-12; 9:10). |
| Altar at Araunah's threshing floor | Jeroboam's golden calves | 1 Kings 11:42–43; 2 Samuel 24:25 | David built the altar in his own reign, and Jeroboam made his calves only after Solomon had reigned forty years and died (2 Samuel 24:25; 1 Kings 11:42-43). |
| Solomon's Temple | Solomon's palace | 1 Kings 9:10; 1 Kings 6:38; 1 Kings 7:1 | The temple was finished in Solomon's eleventh year, and the palace took thirteen years, finished at the end of the twenty years he spent building both houses (1 Kings 6:38; 7:1; 9:10). |
| Solomon's Temple | Jeroboam's golden calves | 1 Kings 12:27–28 | Jeroboam made the calves so that his people would not go up to sacrifice at the house of the LORD in Jerusalem, which was already standing (1 Kings 12:27-28). |
| Solomon's palace | Jeroboam's golden calves | 1 Kings 11:42–43; 1 Kings 9:10 | Solomon finished his palace in his own reign, and Jeroboam made his calves only after Solomon had died (1 Kings 9:10; 11:42-43). |
| Jeroboam's golden calves | Samaria | 1 Kings 16:25–26; 1 Kings 16:24 | Omri, who built Samaria, walked in the ways of Jeroboam and in the sins he had caused Israel to commit, so the calves were already there (1 Kings 16:24-26). |
| Jeroboam's golden calves | Ahab's temple of Baal | 1 Kings 16:30–31; 1 Kings 16:32 | Ahab walked in the sins of Jeroboam, whose calves were already standing, and then built the temple of Baal (1 Kings 16:30-32). |
| Jeroboam's golden calves | Ahab's ivory palace | 1 Kings 16:30–31; 1 Kings 22:39 | When Ahab became king he walked in the sins of Jeroboam, whose calves were already standing, and his ivory palace was built in his own reign (1 Kings 16:30-31; 22:39). |
| Jeroboam's golden calves | Hiel's Jericho | 1 Kings 16:30–31; 1 Kings 16:34 | When Ahab became king he walked in the sins of Jeroboam, whose calves were already standing, and Hiel rebuilt Jericho in Ahab's days (1 Kings 16:30-31, 34). |
| Jeroboam's golden calves | Elijah's altar on Carmel | 1 Kings 16:30–31; 1 Kings 18:1 | When Ahab became king he walked in the sins of Jeroboam, whose calves were already standing, and Elijah built his Carmel altar later in Ahab's reign (1 Kings 16:30-31; 18:1). |
| Samaria | Ahab's temple of Baal | 1 Kings 16:32; 1 Kings 16:24 | Ahab built the temple of Baal in Samaria, the city his father Omri had built (1 Kings 16:24, 32). |
| Samaria | Ahab's ivory palace | 1 Kings 16:28–29; 1 Kings 16:24; 1 Kings 22:39 | Omri built Samaria in his own reign and was buried there, and his son Ahab, who reigned after him, built the ivory palace (1 Kings 16:24, 28-29; 22:39). |
| Samaria | Hiel's Jericho | 1 Kings 16:28–29; 1 Kings 16:24; 1 Kings 16:34 | Omri built Samaria in his own reign and was buried there, and Hiel rebuilt Jericho in the days of his son Ahab, who reigned after him (1 Kings 16:24, 28-29, 34). |
| Samaria | Elijah's altar on Carmel | 1 Kings 16:28–29; 1 Kings 16:24; 1 Kings 18:1 | Omri built Samaria in his own reign and was buried there, and Elijah built his Carmel altar in the reign of Omri's son Ahab (1 Kings 16:24, 28-29; 18:1). |
| Ahaz's Damascus altar | Hezekiah's tunnel | 2 Kings 16:20; 2 Kings 16:10–11; 2 Kings 20:20 | Ahaz had his altar built in his own reign, and Hezekiah, who reigned in his place after Ahaz rested with his fathers, made the tunnel (2 Kings 16:10-11, 20; 20:20). |

### Blocked pairs

| Pair never asked | Why |
|---|---|
| Ahab's temple of Baal and Ahab's ivory palace | Both were built in Ahab's twenty-two years and no verse says which came first. |
| Ahab's temple of Baal and Hiel's Jericho | Both come from Ahab's reign and no verse says which came first. |
| Ahab's temple of Baal and Elijah's altar on Carmel | Both come from Ahab's reign; the Baal temple is told first, but no verse says it was finished before Elijah's altar. |
| Ahab's ivory palace and Hiel's Jericho | Both come from Ahab's reign and no verse says which came first. |
| Ahab's ivory palace and Elijah's altar on Carmel | Both come from Ahab's reign and no verse says which came first. |
| Hiel's Jericho and Elijah's altar on Carmel | Both come from Ahab's reign and no verse says which came first. |
| Ark of the covenant and Tabernacle | The tabernacle's parts are made first in the telling and the ark after (Exodus 36; 37:1), everything is finished and brought to Moses together (Exodus 39:32-43), and Deuteronomy 10:3 has Moses making an ark when he cut the second tablets, so 'finished first' has no single answer. |
| Micah's idol and Gideon's altar | Judges 17-21 is tied to no judge, and Phinehas is still serving (Judges 20:28), so Micah's idol may come before or after Gideon. |
| Micah's idol and Altar by the Jordan | Phinehas is still serving in Judges 20:28 and the Danites had not yet taken their land (Judges 18:1), so Judges 17-21 may reach back to the end of Joshua's days, when the altar by the Jordan was built. |
| Micah's idol and Altar on Mount Ebal | Judges 17-21 may reach back into Joshua's days, and the Ebal passage stands at different points in the Hebrew, Greek and Qumran copies of Joshua, so no verse settles which came first. |
| Gilgal stones and Altar on Mount Ebal | A Qumran copy of Joshua places the altar right after the crossing, and Deuteronomy 27:2 asks for the stones 'on the day you cross the Jordan', so the two may belong to the same days. |
| David's palace and Altar at Araunah's threshing floor | The census story stands in the closing chapters of 2 Samuel, outside the order of David's reign, and Hiram's dates suggest the palace may have been built late in the reign, so either could be first. |
| Gideon's altar and Ebenezer stone | Gideon comes before Samuel in the telling, but the years of the judges may overlap and no single verse sets Gideon's altar against Samuel's stone. |

### Left out

- **Abram's altar at Bethel**: It stands between the Shechem and Hebron altars in Genesis 12:8, and a third Bethel card beside Jacob's pillar would confuse more than it teaches.
- **Jacob's altars at Shechem and at Bethel**: Genesis 33:20 and 35:7 add two more patriarchal altars ordered only by facts; the list keeps Jacob's pillar as his one card.
- **Gideon's ephod**: Judges 8:27 does not say when in Gideon's forty years it was made, and it is not a building.
- **Nebuchadnezzar's statue in his dream**: The statue of Daniel 2 was seen in a dream, never built.

## 5. Where did they go first?

14 journeys, 117 stops, 538 askable pairs (53 easy, 57 medium, 428 hard). A pair is only ever two stops of one journey; the first arrival counts; the starting place is never a card.

### Paul: First journey (Acts 13:1–14:26)

Starts at Antioch of Syria (Acts 13:1–3), never a card.

1. **Seleucia**: Acts 13:4
2. **Salamis**: Acts 13:5
3. **Paphos**: Acts 13:6
4. **Perga**: Acts 13:13
5. **Pisidian Antioch**: Acts 13:14 (no map place)
6. **Iconium**: Acts 13:51
7. **Lystra**: Acts 14:8
8. **Derbe**: Acts 14:20–21
9. **Attalia**: Acts 14:25

### Blocked pairs

| Pair never asked | Why |
|---|---|
| Amphipolis and Apollonia | Acts 17:1 names the two together ('passed through Amphipolis and Apollonia') without saying which came first. |
| Forum of Appius and Three Taverns | Acts 28:15 names the two together as where the brothers came to meet Paul ('as far as the Forum of Appius and the Three Taverns') without ordering them. |

### Left out

- **Cyprus (Paul's first journey)**: The island as a whole (Acts 13:4); its stops, Salamis and Paphos, are cards instead.
- **Pisidia and Pamphylia (Paul's first journey)**: Regions passed through on the way back (Acts 14:24), not single places.
- **Syria and Cilicia (Paul's second journey)**: A region summary (Acts 15:41).
- **Phrygia and Galatia (Paul's second journey)**: A region summary (Acts 16:6).
- **Mysia (Paul's second journey)**: They passed by it (Acts 16:8).
- **Iconium (Paul's second journey)**: Acts 16:2 says only that the brothers there spoke well of Timothy; no verse says Paul came there on this journey.
- **Cenchrea (Paul's second journey)**: Named only for the head-shaving before Paul sailed (Acts 18:18); no verse tells an arrival, and it is a departure point.
- **Jerusalem (Paul's second journey)**: Acts 18:22 in the Greek says only that Paul 'went up and greeted the church'; the city is supplied by the translation, so the stop is not certain.
- **Galatia and Phrygia (Paul's third journey)**: A region summary (Acts 18:23).
- **Macedonia and Greece (Paul's third journey)**: Regions, and their first arrival on this journey is disputed: Paul's letters put Troas before Macedonia (2 Corinthians 2:12-13) and a visit to Corinth from Ephesus probably came before Acts 20:1-2.
- **Philippi (Paul's third journey)**: Named only as the port Paul sailed from (Acts 20:6); when he first reached it in Macedonia is not told, and Troas may have come first (2 Corinthians 2:12-13).
- **Chios (Paul's third journey)**: The ship only arrived 'opposite Chios' (Acts 20:15); it did not put in.
- **Cyprus (Paul's third journey)**: Only sighted and passed (Acts 21:3).
- **Cyprus (Paul's voyage to Rome)**: The ship only sailed to the lee of it (Acts 27:4).
- **Cnidus (Paul's voyage to Rome)**: The ship only 'arrived off Cnidus' and the wind kept it from putting in (Acts 27:7).
- **Crete and Salmone (Paul's voyage to Rome)**: Sailed along, to the lee of Crete and opposite Salmone (Acts 27:7); Fair Havens is the stop there.
- **Phoenix (Paul's voyage to Rome)**: The harbour they hoped to reach and never did (Acts 27:12).
- **Cauda (Paul's voyage to Rome)**: The ship only passed it (Acts 27:16, 'passing to the lee').
- **Kadesh (Israel in the wilderness)**: Israel is at Kadesh early (the spies, Numbers 13:26) and again late (Numbers 20:1; 33:36), and Numbers 33 lists only the late stop.
- **Moseroth and Bene-jaakan (Israel in the wilderness)**: Deuteronomy 10:6 and Numbers 33:30-31 give them in opposite orders.
- **Taberah (Israel in the wilderness)**: Named in the story (Numbers 11:3) but not in the Numbers 33 itinerary, so whether it is a separate stop before Kibroth-hattaavah is not certain.
- **The Desert of Shur and the Wilderness of Paran (Israel in the wilderness)**: Wide regions rather than camps; Israel was in Paran at more than one time (Numbers 10:12; 12:16; 13:26).
- **The Red Sea camp (Israel in the wilderness)**: Numbers 33:10 has Israel camp by the Red Sea after Elim, which a player would confuse with the crossing at Pi-hahiroth.
- **Stops after Iye-abarim (Israel in the wilderness)**: Numbers 21:12-20 (Zered, the Arnon, Beer, Mattanah, Nahaliel, Bamoth) and Numbers 33:45-47 (Dibon-gad, Almon-diblathaim, the mountains of Abarim) name different places, so how they interleave is not certain.
- **Other camps known only from Numbers 33 (Israel in the wilderness)**: Dophkah, Alush, Rithmah to Abronah, Zalmonah and Punon are very obscure names from the list alone; Jotbathah also appears in Deuteronomy 10:7 on a route that may be a different trip. Left out to keep the journey certain and playable.
- **The Negev and the land of Canaan (Abraham)**: Regions (Genesis 12:5, 12:9; 13:1; 20:1).
- **Dan and Hobah (Abraham)**: A pursuit to rescue Lot (Genesis 14:14-15), not a place Abraham went to live or camp; 'Dan' is also the later name of the town.
- **The hill country of Gilead, Galeed and Mizpah (Jacob)**: Gilead is a region (Genesis 31:25), and the heap there has two names, Galeed and Mizpah (31:47-49), which would make a confusing card.
- **The ford of the Jabbok (Jacob)**: A river crossing on the way to Peniel (Genesis 32:22); Peniel is the stop there.
- **The Tower of Eder (Jacob)**: Jacob pitched his tent beyond it (Genesis 35:21), not at it.
- **Goshen (Jacob)**: Part of Egypt (Genesis 46:28); Egypt is the stop.
- **Abel-meholah (Elijah)**: It is Elisha's home (1 Kings 19:16), but 19:19 does not say where Elijah found him, so no verse places Elijah there.
- **Samaria (Elijah)**: 1 Kings 18:2 says the famine was severe in Samaria; it does not place Elijah there.
- **The Desert of Damascus (Elijah)**: Only commanded (1 Kings 19:15); no verse tells Elijah going there.
- **Baale of Judah, the house of Abinadab (the ark of the covenant)**: The same place as Kiriath-jearim (1 Samuel 7:1; 2 Samuel 6:2-3), not a new stop.
- **The towns between Azotus and Caesarea (Philip)**: An unnamed summary (Acts 8:40).
- **Jonathan's meeting place (David fleeing Saul)**: 1 Samuel 20 names no town for where David met Jonathan.
- **The stronghold (David fleeing Saul)**: Not named (1 Samuel 22:4-5); it is unclear whether it is Adullam or a place in Moab.
- **Horesh and the hill of Hachilah (David fleeing Saul)**: Spots inside the Wilderness of Ziph (1 Samuel 23:15, 19; 26:1-3), which is the stop.
- **The Wilderness of Paran (David fleeing Saul)**: 1 Samuel 25:1 reads Paran in the Hebrew but Maon in the Greek, so the stop is not certain.
- **Carmel (David fleeing Saul)**: Nabal's town (1 Samuel 25:2); no verse places David in it.

## 6. Who died first?

48 items, 1,039 askable pairs (172 easy, 523 medium, 344 hard).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Abel | Adam's second son | early Genesis: no year shown; ordered only by verses among early-Genesis items | Timeline event cain-kills-abel | Genesis 4:8 | Killed by his brother Cain in the first family, before Seth was born (Genesis 4:8, 25). |
| Adam | The first man | early Genesis: no year shown; ordered only by verses among early-Genesis items | Who's older? birth window of adam-gen-2-19 plus 930 to 930 years | Genesis 5:5 | Died at 930 (Genesis 5:5). |
| Methuselah | Longest life in the Bible | early Genesis: no year shown; ordered only by verses among early-Genesis items | Who's older? birth window of methuselah-gen-5-21 plus 969 to 969 years | Genesis 5:27 | Died at 969 (Genesis 5:27), in the year of the flood by the Bible's own numbers. |
| Noah | Builder of the ark | early Genesis: no year shown; ordered only by verses among early-Genesis items | Who's older? birth window of noah-gen-5-29 plus 950 to 950 years | Genesis 9:29 | Died at 950, 350 years after the flood (Genesis 9:28–29). |
| Sarah | Abraham's wife | about 2078 BC to 1576 BC | Who's older? birth window of sarah-gen-11-29 plus 127 to 127 years | Genesis 23:2 | Died at 127 in Hebron, when Isaac was 37 (Genesis 23:1–2). |
| Abraham | Patriarch | about 2040 BC to 1538 BC | Who's older? birth window of abraham-gen-11-26 plus 175 to 175 years | Genesis 25:8 | Died at 175 (Genesis 25:7–8). |
| Ishmael | Abraham's son by Hagar | about 1992 BC to 1490 BC | Who's older? birth window of ishmael-gen-16-11 plus 137 to 137 years | Genesis 25:17 | Died at 137 (Genesis 25:17). |

### Order facts

| Came first | Then | Verse | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| Abel | Adam | Genesis 4:25; Genesis 5:5 | Seth was born to Adam 'in place of Abel, since Cain killed him', and Adam lived on to 930. |
| Abel | Methuselah | Genesis 4:25; Genesis 5:21 | Abel was already dead when Seth was born, and Methuselah came generations later in Seth's line. |
| Abel | Noah | Genesis 4:25; Genesis 5:29 | Abel was already dead when Seth was born, and Noah came generations later in Seth's line. |
| Adam | Methuselah | Genesis 5:27; Genesis 5:5; Genesis 5:21 | Methuselah, born generations after Adam, lived 969 years, longer than Adam's 930, so he outlived him. |
| Adam | Noah | Genesis 9:29; Genesis 5:5 | Noah, born generations after Adam, lived 950 years, longer than Adam's 930, so he outlived him. |
| Methuselah | Noah | Genesis 5:25–27; Genesis 5:28; Genesis 7:6; Genesis 9:28–29 | Methuselah was 187 when Lamech was born and Lamech 182 when Noah was born, so Methuselah's 969 years ended in Noah's 600th year, the year of the flood; Noah lived 350 years after the flood. |
| Sarah | Abraham | Genesis 23:1–2; Genesis 25:7–8 | Sarah died at 127 and Abraham mourned her; he lived on to 175. |
| Sarah | Isaac | Genesis 24:67; Genesis 35:28–29 | Isaac 'was comforted after his mother's death' when he married Rebekah. |
| Sarah | Ishmael | Genesis 23:2; Genesis 25:9 | Sarah died before Abraham, and Ishmael lived to help bury Abraham. |
| Sarah | Rebekah | Genesis 24:67; Genesis 49:31 | Isaac married Rebekah after his mother Sarah's death. |

| Sarah | Joseph | Genesis 24:67; Genesis 25:26; Genesis 50:26 | Sarah died before her grandson Jacob was born, and Joseph was Jacob's son. |

| John the Baptist | Sapphira | Matthew 14:10–12; Acts 5:10 | John was beheaded during Jesus' ministry, and Sapphira died later in the church at Jerusalem. |
| John the Baptist | Stephen | Matthew 14:10–12; Acts 7:59–60 | John was beheaded during Jesus' ministry, and Stephen was stoned later as a leader in the church at Jerusalem. |
| Judas Iscariot | Ananias | Matthew 27:3–5; Acts 1:18; Acts 5:5 | Judas hanged himself when Jesus was condemned, and Ananias died later in the church that began at Pentecost. |
| Judas Iscariot | Sapphira | Matthew 27:3–5; Acts 1:18; Acts 5:10 | Judas hanged himself when Jesus was condemned, and Sapphira died later in the church that began at Pentecost. |
| Judas Iscariot | Stephen | Matthew 27:3–5; Acts 1:18; Acts 7:59–60 | Judas hanged himself when Jesus was condemned, and Stephen was stoned later as a leader in the church. |
| Ananias | Sapphira | Acts 5:9–10; Acts 5:5; Acts 5:7 | About three hours after Ananias died, Peter told Sapphira, 'the feet of the men who buried your husband are at the door'. |
| James son of Zebedee | Herod Agrippa I | Acts 12:1–2; Acts 12:23 | King Herod had James put to death with the sword, and was later struck down himself. |

### Blocked pairs

| Pair never asked | Why |
|---|---|
| Saul and Jonathan | They died the same day in the same battle on Mount Gilboa (1 Samuel 31:2–6). |
| Eli and Samson | Samson's twenty years fell within the Philistines' forty years, which may overlap Eli's forty years as judge, so which of them died first is not settled. |
| Rebekah and Isaac | No verse settles it: Genesis 49:31 says both were buried before Jacob died, and gives no order. |
| Rebekah and Rachel | Scripture never says when Rebekah died, only that she was buried before Jacob's death (Genesis 49:31). |
| Rebekah and Ishmael | Scripture never says when Rebekah died, so her death against Ishmael's is not settled. |
| Ishmael and Rachel | Ishmael died when Jacob was 63; Rachel died after Jacob's twenty years with Laban, but Scripture does not give Jacob's age when he left home, so either could have died first. |
| Samuel and Nabal | 1 Samuel 25 tells Samuel's death just before Nabal's story, but does not say that Nabal died after Samuel. |

### Left out

- **Terah**: Genesis 11:26, 11:32 and Acts 7:4 leave Abraham's birth year against Terah's open by sixty years, so Terah's death against Abraham's move and the deaths near it is not settled.
- **Lamech (Noah's father)**: The Hebrew numbers put his death five years before Methuselah's, but the Greek and Samaritan numbers differ.
- **Nadab and Abihu**: They died at the same moment (Leviticus 10:2), and either alone adds little to the game.
- **Korah, Dathan and Abiram**: They died in the same event (Numbers 16:31–35).
- **Abinadab and Malchishua**: Saul's sons died the same day as Saul and Jonathan (1 Samuel 31:2–6), so every pair among them would be blocked.

## 7. Which comes first in your Bible?

66 books in the site's order (Genesis to Revelation); nothing to review.

## 8. Bible Firsts

61 questions. Each has one right answer and two or three wrong ones; "never" names a defensible answer that is never offered.

1. **Who was the first man?** Answer: **Adam**. Wrong options: Cain; Seth; Noah.
   Scripture states it: 1 Corinthians 15:45; Genesis 2:7. Paul writes, 'The first man Adam became a living being,' recalling how the LORD God formed man from the dust of the ground (Genesis 2:7).
2. **Who was the first woman?** Answer: **Eve**. Wrong options: Sarah; Adah; Leah.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Genesis 3:20; 1 Timothy 2:13. Adam named his wife Eve 'because she would be the mother of all the living', and Paul writes, 'Adam was formed first, and then Eve.'
3. **Whose death is the first one the Bible tells?** Answer: **Abel**. Wrong options: Adam; Seth; Methuselah.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Genesis 4:8. Genesis tells no death before Cain 'rose up against his brother Abel and killed him' (Genesis 4:8); Adam's death comes later, in Genesis 5:5.
4. **Who built the first city the Bible mentions?** Answer: **Cain**. Wrong options: Nimrod; Seth; Jabal. Never offered: Enoch.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Genesis 4:17. The first city in the Bible is the one in Genesis 4:17: 'Cain built a city and named it after his son Enoch.'
5. **After whose birth does Genesis say people began to call on the name of the LORD?** Answer: **Enosh**. Wrong options: Kenan; Noah; Methuselah. Never offered: Seth; Adam.
   Scripture states it: Genesis 4:26. 'To Seth also a son was born, and he called him Enosh. At that time men began to call upon the name of the LORD.'
6. **Who was the father of all who play the harp and flute?** Answer: **Jubal**. Wrong options: Jabal; Tubal-cain; Asaph. Never offered: Lamech.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Genesis 4:20–22. Genesis 4:21 says Jubal 'was the father of all who play the harp and flute'; his brother Jabal fathered tent-dwellers and Tubal-cain forged bronze and iron.
7. **Which bird did Noah send out first?** Answer: **A raven**. Wrong options: A dove; A swallow; An eagle.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Genesis 8:6–8. Noah opened the window of the ark 'and sent out a raven'; only then did he send out a dove (Genesis 8:7-8).
8. **Who was Isaac's firstborn son?** Answer: **Esau**. Wrong options: Jacob; Ishmael; Joseph. Never offered: Edom.
   Scripture states it: Genesis 25:25–26; Genesis 27:32. 'The first one came out red, covered with hair like a fur coat; so they named him Esau,' and Jacob came out after him, grasping his heel.
9. **Who was Jacob's firstborn son?** Answer: **Reuben**. Wrong options: Judah; Simeon; Levi. Never offered: Joseph.
   Scripture states it: Genesis 49:3; Genesis 29:32. Jacob told him, 'Reuben, you are my firstborn, my might, and the beginning of my strength' (Genesis 49:3).
10. **Whom did the LORD raise up as the first deliverer in the book of Judges?** Answer: **Othniel**. Wrong options: Ehud; Deborah; Gideon.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Judges 3:9–10; Judges 3:15. The first time Israel cried out in Judges, the LORD 'raised up Othniel son of Caleb's younger brother Kenaz as a deliverer to save them'; Ehud came next (Judges 3:15).
11. **Who was Israel's first high priest?** Answer: **Aaron**. Wrong options: Eleazar; Phinehas; Eli. Never offered: Melchizedek; Moses.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Exodus 28:1; Hebrews 5:1–4; Numbers 20:28. God called Aaron and his sons 'to serve Me as priests' (Exodus 28:1), and Hebrews says a high priest 'must be called by God, just as Aaron was'; Eleazar took his garments only when Aaron died.
12. **Who was the first king of Israel?** Answer: **Saul**. Wrong options: David; Samuel; Ish-bosheth. Never offered: Abimelech.
   Scripture plainly shows it: 1 Samuel 10:1; 1 Samuel 10:24; Acts 13:21. When the people asked for a king, Samuel anointed Saul and all the people shouted, 'Long live the king!' (1 Samuel 10:24; Acts 13:21).
13. **When Solomon's kingdom split, who became the first king of the northern tribes?** Answer: **Jeroboam**. Wrong options: Omri; Ahab; Baasha. Never offered: Ish-bosheth.
   Scripture plainly shows it: 1 Kings 12:19–20. When Israel rebelled against the house of David, they summoned Jeroboam 'and made him king over all Israel' (1 Kings 12:20).
14. **When Solomon's kingdom split, who was Judah's first king?** Answer: **Rehoboam**. Wrong options: Abijah; Asa; Jehoshaphat. Never offered: David; Solomon.
   Scripture plainly shows it: 1 Kings 12:17; 1 Kings 14:21. After the split, 'Rehoboam still reigned over the Israelites living in the cities of Judah' (1 Kings 12:17); Rehoboam son of Solomon reigned seventeen years in Jerusalem.
15. **Who was King David's firstborn son?** Answer: **Amnon**. Wrong options: Absalom; Solomon; Adonijah.
   Scripture states it: 2 Samuel 3:2–4. Of the sons born to David in Hebron, 'His firstborn was Amnon'; Absalom was the third and Adonijah the fourth (2 Samuel 3:2-4).
16. **What does John call the first of Jesus's signs?** Answer: **Water into wine**. Wrong options: Healing an official's son; Feeding the five thousand; Walking on water.
   Scripture states it: John 2:9–11; John 4:54. After the water was turned into wine, John writes, 'Jesus performed this, the first of His signs, at Cana in Galilee'; the official's son was 'the second sign' (John 4:54).
17. **Whom did Andrew first find and tell about Jesus?** Answer: **His brother Simon**. Wrong options: Philip; Nathanael; Thomas.
   Scripture states it: John 1:40–42. Andrew 'first found his brother Simon and told him, "We have found the Messiah"', and brought him to Jesus (John 1:41-42).
18. **Which apostle does Matthew name first in his list of the Twelve?** Answer: **Simon Peter**. Wrong options: Andrew; John; Matthew.
   Scripture states it: Matthew 10:2. 'These are the names of the twelve apostles: first Simon, called Peter, and his brother Andrew' (Matthew 10:2).
19. **When Peter and the disciple whom Jesus loved ran to the tomb, who got there first?** Answer: **The disciple Jesus loved**. Wrong options: Peter; They came together. Never offered: Mary Magdalene; John.
   Scripture states it: John 20:2–4. 'The two were running together, but the other disciple outran Peter and reached the tomb first' (John 20:4); Mary Magdalene had been there before them both (John 20:1).
20. **Which did Jesus call the first and greatest commandment?** Answer: **Love the Lord your God**. Wrong options: Love your neighbor as yourself; Honor your father and mother; Keep the Sabbath holy. Never offered: You shall have no other gods.
   Scripture states it: Matthew 22:37–39. Jesus said, 'Love the Lord your God with all your heart and with all your soul and with all your mind. This is the first and greatest commandment,' and loving your neighbor is the second.
21. **Which does Paul call the first commandment with a promise?** Answer: **Honor your father and mother**. Wrong options: You shall not murder; You shall not steal; Keep the Sabbath holy. Never offered: You shall not make an idol.
   Scripture states it: Ephesians 6:2–3; Exodus 20:12. Paul writes, 'Honor your father and mother (which is the first commandment with a promise), that it may go well with you and that you may have a long life on the earth.'
22. **Where were the disciples first called Christians?** Answer: **Antioch**. Wrong options: Jerusalem; Rome; Ephesus.
   Scripture states it: Acts 11:26. 'The disciples were first called Christians at Antioch' (Acts 11:26).
23. **At the Jerusalem council, James said God had first visited the Gentiles. In whose house?** Answer: **Cornelius**. Wrong options: Lydia; Sergius Paulus; The Philippian jailer. Never offered: The Ethiopian official.
   Scripture states it: Acts 15:14; Acts 15:7; Acts 10:24; Acts 10:44–48. James said, 'Simon has told us how God first visited the Gentiles,' recalling how Peter preached in Cornelius's house and the Holy Spirit fell on the Gentiles there (Acts 10:44-48).
24. **Whose baptism in Philippi does Acts tell first?** Answer: **Lydia**. Wrong options: The jailer; Euodia; Epaphroditus.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Acts 16:14–15; Acts 16:33. In Philippi, Acts first tells how Lydia 'and her household had been baptized' (Acts 16:15); the jailer and his household were baptized later that same chapter (16:33).
25. **Who was the first Christian martyr?** Answer: **Stephen**. Wrong options: James, brother of John; Philip; Barnabas. Never offered: John the Baptist; Jesus.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Acts 7:59–60; Acts 8:1; Acts 22:20; Acts 12:2. Stephen was stoned while he prayed, 'Lord Jesus, receive my spirit' (Acts 7:59–60), and Paul later spoke of the day 'the blood of Your witness Stephen was shed' (22:20). James the brother of John was put to death only later (Acts 12:2).
26. **What are the first words of the Bible?** Answer: **In the beginning**. Wrong options: The beginning of the gospel; Blessed is the man; Hear, O Israel. Never offered: In the beginning was the Word.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Genesis 1:1. The Bible opens, 'In the beginning God created the heavens and the earth' (Genesis 1:1). John's gospel opens the same way, but it is not the Bible's first verse.
27. **Who was Adam and Eve's first son?** Answer: **Cain**. Wrong options: Abel; Seth; Enoch.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Genesis 4:1–2. Eve 'gave birth to Cain', and 'later she gave birth to Cain's brother Abel' (Genesis 4:1–2); Seth was born after Abel's death (4:25).
28. **Who was Abraham's first son?** Answer: **Ishmael**. Wrong options: Isaac; Midian; Jacob.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Genesis 16:15–16; Genesis 21:5; Genesis 15:3. Abram had no child of his own (Genesis 15:3) until Hagar bore Ishmael when he was eighty-six (16:15–16); Isaac was born when he was a hundred (21:5).
29. **Who was Judah's firstborn?** Answer: **Er**. Wrong options: Onan; Shelah; Perez.
   Scripture states it: 1 Chronicles 2:3; Genesis 38:6–7. 'The sons of Judah: Er, Onan, and Shelah ... Er, Judah's firstborn, was wicked in the sight of the LORD' (1 Chronicles 2:3; Genesis 38:6–7).
30. **One of Judah's twin sons put out his hand first and got the scarlet thread. Which twin was born first?** Answer: **Perez**. Wrong options: Zerah; Shelah.
   Scripture states it: Genesis 38:28–30. The midwife tied a scarlet thread on the hand that came out, but that twin pulled it back and his brother came out: 'You have broken out first!' So he was named Perez; Zerah, with the thread, came after (Genesis 38:28–30).
31. **Who was Joseph's firstborn?** Answer: **Manasseh**. Wrong options: Ephraim; Benjamin; Machir.
   Scripture states it: Genesis 41:51; Genesis 48:14; Genesis 48:18. 'Joseph named the firstborn Manasseh' (Genesis 41:51); Jacob crossed his hands to bless Ephraim, the younger, 'although Manasseh was the firstborn' (48:14).
32. **Who was Aaron's firstborn?** Answer: **Nadab**. Wrong options: Abihu; Eleazar; Ithamar.
   Scripture states it: Numbers 3:2; Exodus 6:23. 'These are the names of the sons of Aaron: Nadab the firstborn, then Abihu, Eleazar, and Ithamar' (Numbers 3:2).
33. **Who was Jesse's firstborn?** Answer: **Eliab**. Wrong options: David; Abinadab; Shammah.
   Scripture states it: 1 Chronicles 2:13; 1 Samuel 17:13–14. 'Jesse was the father of Eliab his firstborn' (1 Chronicles 2:13); when Israel faced Goliath, 'the firstborn was Eliab', 'and David was the youngest' (1 Samuel 17:13–14).
34. **Whom does Luke call Mary's firstborn son?** Answer: **Jesus**. Wrong options: James; Joses; John the Baptist.
   Scripture states it: Luke 2:7. 'She gave birth to her firstborn, a Son. She wrapped Him in swaddling cloths and laid Him in a manger' (Luke 2:7). James and Joses are named among Jesus's brothers (Mark 6:3).
35. **Which tribe did God take in place of Israel's firstborn?** Answer: **Levi**. Wrong options: Judah; Reuben; Benjamin.
   Scripture states it: Numbers 3:12; Numbers 3:45. 'I have taken the Levites from among the children of Israel in place of every firstborn Israelite from the womb' (Numbers 3:12; again in 3:45).
36. **Who asked the first question in the Bible?** Answer: **The serpent**. Wrong options: God; Eve; Adam.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Genesis 3:1. Genesis 1 and 2 hold no question; the first is the serpent's to the woman: 'Did God really say, "You must not eat from any tree in the garden?"' (Genesis 3:1).
37. **What was God's first question to Adam after he ate?** Answer: **Where are you?**. Wrong options: Who told you that you were naked?; What is this you have done?; Where is your brother?.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Genesis 3:9. After they ate, 'the LORD God called out to the man, "Where are you?"' (Genesis 3:9); 'Who told you that you were naked?' came after (3:11).
38. **Who ate the forbidden fruit first?** Answer: **Eve**. Wrong options: Adam; They ate together; The serpent.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Genesis 3:6. The woman 'took the fruit and ate it. She also gave some to her husband who was with her, and he ate it' (Genesis 3:6).
39. **Whom did Jacob marry first?** Answer: **Leah**. Wrong options: Rachel; Bilhah; Zilpah.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Genesis 29:23; Genesis 29:25–28. Laban gave Jacob Leah first: 'It is not our custom here to give the younger daughter in marriage before the older.' Rachel was given to him after the week's celebration (Genesis 29:23–28).
40. **What was the first sign God gave Moses at the burning bush?** Answer: **His staff became a snake**. Wrong options: His hand became leprous; Water turned to blood; Aaron's staff budded.
   Scripture states it: Exodus 4:2–4; Exodus 4:8. Moses threw his staff on the ground 'and it became a snake' (Exodus 4:3). Then came his leprous hand, and the LORD spoke of 'the first sign' and 'the second' (4:6–8); water turning to blood was a third (4:9).
41. **Which month did God make the first month of Israel's year?** Answer: **The month of the Passover (Abib)**. Wrong options: The month of the Feast of Tabernacles; The month of the Day of Atonement; The month of Pentecost.
   Scripture states it: Exodus 12:2; Exodus 12:11; Exodus 13:4. Giving the first Passover, the LORD said, 'This month is the beginning of months for you; it shall be the first month of your year' (Exodus 12:2, 11); Israel left Egypt 'in the month of Abib' (13:4).
42. **Where did Israel first camp after crossing the Jordan?** Answer: **Gilgal**. Wrong options: Jericho; Shiloh; Bethel.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Joshua 4:19. 'On the tenth day of the first month the people went up from the Jordan and camped at Gilgal on the eastern border of Jericho' (Joshua 4:19).
43. **Which city did Israel conquer first in Canaan?** Answer: **Jericho**. Wrong options: Ai; Hebron; Hazor.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Joshua 6:2; Joshua 6:20. Israel's first city taken in Canaan was Jericho: the wall collapsed 'and all the people charged straight into the city and captured it' (Joshua 6:20). Ai fell after (Joshua 7–8).
44. **On Gideon's first night with the fleece, what happened?** Answer: **The fleece was wet and the ground dry**. Wrong options: The fleece was dry and the ground wet; Both were dry.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Judges 6:37–40. Gideon asked for dew only on the fleece with all the ground dry, 'and that is what happened'; the next night he asked for the reverse (Judges 6:37–40).
45. **What was the devil's first temptation of Jesus?** Answer: **To turn stones into bread**. Wrong options: To throw himself down from the temple; To bow down and worship him; To turn water into wine.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Matthew 4:3; Luke 4:3. In both Matthew and Luke the tempter first says, 'If You are the Son of God, tell these stones to become bread' (Matthew 4:3; Luke 4:3). The two gospels give the other temptations in different orders.
46. **In the story of Jesus's life, what are the first words the Bible records Him saying?** Answer: **Why were you looking for Me? Did you not know that I had to be in My Father's house?**. Wrong options: Man shall not live on bread alone; Let it be so now (at His baptism); Follow Me.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Luke 2:49; Luke 2:42. The first words of Jesus any gospel records come when He was twelve, in the temple (Luke 2:42, 49). His words at His baptism and in the wilderness came when He was about thirty (Luke 3:23).
47. **Two of John the Baptist's disciples first followed Jesus. Which one does John name?** Answer: **Andrew**. Wrong options: Peter; Philip; Nathanael.
   Scripture plainly shows it: John 1:37; John 1:40–41. When the two disciples heard John, 'they followed Jesus'; 'Andrew, Simon Peter's brother, was one of the two', and he then found his brother Simon (John 1:37, 40–41). The other is not named.
48. **When Peter and the other disciple ran to the tomb, which of them went inside first?** Answer: **Simon Peter**. Wrong options: The other disciple; They went in together.
   Scripture plainly shows it: John 20:5–6; John 20:8. The other disciple reached the tomb first but did not go in; 'Simon Peter arrived just after him. He entered the tomb', and only then did the other disciple go in (John 20:5–8).
49. **On which day of the week did Jesus rise?** Answer: **The first day of the week**. Wrong options: The seventh day (the Sabbath); The sixth day; The second day.
   Scripture states it: John 20:1; Matthew 28:1; Mark 16:2; Mark 16:6. 'Early on the first day of the week' Mary Magdalene found the stone removed (John 20:1); Matthew and Mark also put the empty tomb 'on the first day of the week', where the women heard, 'He has risen!' (Matthew 28:1; Mark 16:2, 6).
50. **What did Jesus say to seek first?** Answer: **The kingdom of God and His righteousness**. Wrong options: Food and clothing; Peace with everyone; The approval of others.
   Scripture states it: Matthew 6:33. 'But seek first the kingdom of God and His righteousness, and all these things will be added unto you' (Matthew 6:33).
51. **Before taking the speck out of your brother's eye, what must you first take out?** Answer: **The beam (log) in your own eye**. Wrong options: The speck in your own eye; Your brother's fault.
   Scripture states it: Matthew 7:5. 'You hypocrite! First take the beam out of your own eye, and then you will see clearly to remove the speck from your brother's eye' (Matthew 7:5).
52. **Before offering your gift at the altar, what did Jesus say to do first?** Answer: **Be reconciled to your brother**. Wrong options: Pray for him; Wash your hands; Pay your vows.
   Scripture states it: Matthew 5:23–24. 'Leave your gift there before the altar. First go and be reconciled to your brother; then come and offer your gift' (Matthew 5:24).
53. **How does John finish the words "We love because..."?** Answer: **He first loved us**. Wrong options: It is commanded; Love never fails; Others love us.
   Scripture states it: 1 John 4:19. 'We love because He first loved us' (1 John 4:19).
54. **The gospel is the power of God for salvation, first to whom?** Answer: **The Jew**. Wrong options: The Greek; The Roman; The poor.
   Scripture states it: Romans 1:16. Paul calls the gospel 'the power of God for salvation to everyone who believes, first to the Jew, then to the Greek' (Romans 1:16).
55. **When the Lord comes down from heaven, who will rise first?** Answer: **The dead in Christ**. Wrong options: Those who are still alive; The angels; The apostles.
   Scripture states it: 1 Thessalonians 4:16–17. 'The Lord Himself will descend from heaven ... and the dead in Christ will be the first to rise. After that, we who are alive and remain will be caught up together with them' (1 Thessalonians 4:16–17).
56. **Whom does Paul call "the firstfruits of those who have fallen asleep"?** Answer: **Christ**. Wrong options: Adam; Abel; Stephen.
   Scripture states it: 1 Corinthians 15:20. 'But Christ has indeed been raised from the dead, the firstfruits of those who have fallen asleep' (1 Corinthians 15:20).
57. **In Revelation, who says "I am the First and the Last"?** Answer: **Jesus**. Wrong options: John; An angel; Michael.
   Scripture states it: Revelation 1:17–18; Revelation 22:13; Revelation 22:16. The one who tells John 'I am the First and the Last' goes on, 'I was dead, and behold, now I am alive forever' (Revelation 1:17–18); at the end He says it again and names Himself: 'I, Jesus' (22:13, 16).
58. **Which church in Revelation abandoned the love it had at first?** Answer: **Ephesus**. Wrong options: Laodicea; Sardis; Smyrna.
   Scripture states it: Revelation 2:1; Revelation 2:4. To the church in Ephesus: 'I have this against you: You have abandoned your first love' (Revelation 2:1, 4).
59. **What is the first Beatitude?** Answer: **Blessed are the poor in spirit**. Wrong options: Blessed are those who mourn; Blessed are the meek; Blessed are the pure in heart.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Matthew 5:3–4. Jesus's blessings in the Sermon on the Mount begin, 'Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven' (Matthew 5:3); 'Blessed are those who mourn' comes next.
60. **Which fruit of the Spirit does Paul name first?** Answer: **Love**. Wrong options: Joy; Peace; Faithfulness.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Galatians 5:22. 'But the fruit of the Spirit is love, joy, peace, patience, kindness, goodness, faithfulness' (Galatians 5:22).
61. **Which of the seven churches in Revelation is addressed first?** Answer: **Ephesus**. Wrong options: Smyrna; Laodicea; Philadelphia.
   Scripture plainly shows it: Revelation 2:1; Revelation 1:11. John is told to send his scroll 'to Ephesus, Smyrna, Pergamum, Thyatira, Sardis, Philadelphia, and Laodicea' (Revelation 1:11), and the first letter begins, 'To the angel of the church in Ephesus write' (2:1).

### Left out

- **Who first saw the risen Jesus?**: Mark 16:9 states it, but that verse is in Mark's longer ending, whose place in Mark is disputed, and the other gospels do not settle who saw him first.
- **Who was the first prophet?**: Abraham is the first person called a prophet (Genesis 20:7), but that is only the first mention, not a stated first.
- **Who was the first mighty one on the earth? (Nimrod)**: The Berean says Nimrod 'began to be a mighty one' (Genesis 10:8), with no 'first', and Genesis 6:4 names 'mighty men of old' before the Flood.
- **Who was the first apostle to die?**: Acts records only James's death among the apostles (Acts 12:2), and Judas Iscariot died before him (Matthew 27:5; Acts 1:18), so any answer rests on inference about who counts.
- **Who planted the first vineyard? (Noah)**: The Berean says only that Noah 'proceeded to plant a vineyard' (Genesis 9:20); there is no 'first', and it is only the first mention.
- **What was made on each day of Creation?**: The owner ruled out every question on the days of Creation.
- **Which plague came first?**: The owner ruled out every question on the order of the plagues.
- **Which book of the Bible was written first?**: The order of writing rests on scholarship, not on a verse, and the owner ruled it out.
- **Which of Jesus's miracles came first?**: The gospels do not give one order for his miracles, and the owner ruled it out; only John's 'first of His signs' (John 2:11) is asked.
